## Direction

Recorded direction, not a claim that the path exists or has passed acceptance:

- Preserve the old Lightsail host's evidence.
- Keep the old host unproven. Never describe it as clean.
- Plan a replacement deployed from a known Git commit and a reviewed environment.
- Use native systemd services instead of PM2 resurrect.
- Require application behavior, backup, and restore verification before acceptance.
- Keep old-host retirement planned until it is separately authorized and observed.

**Objective.** A maintainable staging and production path for `ainow.biz`. This registration records that objective. It does not create the path and it does not accept one.

The consolidated plan's four amendments stay in force: console stop of the whole old instance after preservation; restore must show usable application state with external execution and email disabled, and rollback must be schema-compatible; the external inventory is bounded, and consequential gaps need an explicit decision; retention starts at 30 days, earlier deletion is conditional, and an extension needs explicit cost approval.

`status=READY` together with `startCondition=NOT_READY` is valid registration encoding. It does not grant execution permission. Each implementation candidate uses that pair because this registration does not grant later authorization. Changing `startCondition` to `READY` is a later control-plane act and is not authorized now.

`scripts/validate-lane-capacity.ps1` `Test-Admissible` checks `status` and `startCondition` first. An OPTIONAL candidate then returns `NOT_FORCING`. Only a FORCING candidate continues to dependency and runtime-authorization checks. A saturation PASS does not prove DEPLOY-01 dependency satisfaction, host authorization, or provider authorization. Explicit control-plane checks against each slice's recorded prerequisites remain necessary. A saturation PASS is not admission, implementation acceptance, or host authorization.

`CLEAN-BASELINE-DEPLOY-01` is `OPTIONAL`. `CLEAN-BASELINE-RUNTIME-01` and `CLEAN-BASELINE-OPS-01` are `FORCING`. RUNTIME-01 and OPS-01 do not share a mutex: OPS-01 lists none. `localRuntimeAuthorized` is already true in the sidecar. `stagingAuthorized`, `providerLiveAuthorized`, and `creditAuthorized` stay false. The true local-runtime flag does not prove that a local runtime capability check has been performed.

RUNTIME-01's mutexes are `CONTAINER-MANAGER`, `LOCAL-RUNTIME`, and `ENV`. `ENV` is the catalog matcher required because `services/container-manager/.env.example` has basename prefix `.env` (`mutex-catalog.json`, ENV, `exclusive: true`). It is not a `runtimeNeeds` entry and it is not authorization to edit environment files, read secrets, or touch a host. RUNTIME-01 and DEPLOY-01 both declare `ENV`, so they are not a concurrent pair. DEPLOY-01's slice gates are unchanged.

## RUNTIME-01 and OPS-01 acceptance bounds (not executed)

RUNTIME-01 implementation requires GOV-01 completion/lock and AUTH-1. Acceptance requires successful compatibility evidence for the supported runtime selected on the approved path: Node 24, or Node 22 if Node 24 fails. The consolidated plan §6.R builds, test suites, and workspace-bootstrap verification remain required. A failed check, an attempted check, or a stop for a PACKAGE task is BLOCKED / pending evidence. It is not acceptance and it is not grounds for LOCK. The selected host runtime, workspace image, and documented configuration must stay consistent with that approved fallback outcome.

OPS-01 implementation requires GOV-01 completion/lock and AUTH-1. Sidecar `mutexes=[]` and `runtimeNeeds=[]` cover code preparation and static checks only. Before any runtime test, the control plane must update the actual resource and evidence needs, including LOCAL-RUNTIME when the test uses it. Runtime work that shares LOCAL-RUNTIME is sequential with RUNTIME-01. Local runtime capability is still to be established. Its absence is not already proven, and the sidecar flag `localRuntimeAuthorized=true` is not that proof. Static checks alone cannot satisfy the focused operational tests and cannot justify OPS-01 LOCK. Governing corrections a-d, U1, and U2 remain in force. No implementation test or capability probe is authorized now.

## Governing corrections (binding on OPS-01; not implemented)

These correct the addendum's operational sequences. OPS-01 acceptance requires them. This registration does not mark them done.

**a. Phase names and restart order.** Implementation must use one phase vocabulary across prepare, first-start, restore, deploy, backup, and maint, and every recovery path may name only phases the scripts write. Any authorized writer restart removes the maintenance marker first, then starts the unit. Addendum §3.5 Q3 currently restarts `aisandbox-gateway` and `aisandbox-frontend` and then removes the marker. That order cannot succeed while those units assert that the marker is absent. The required Q3 order is: confirm the tree and schema are unchanged, remove the marker, then start the two units.

**b. Pre-start and post-start failure.** Pre-start failure means the marker is still present and no writer has started in this operation. Post-start failure means the marker has been removed or a writer start is already in the journal, including a start that then crashed. After startup has occurred, records must not say that writers never started. Interruption after marker removal, including reboot, is in scope: with the marker gone, enabled units can start at boot. Implementation must define that case and test the documented recovery.

**c. Restore reproduces the backed-up file set.** Restore must remove tracked files that the prepared checkout contains and the backup does not, so a tracked file deleted on the source does not reappear. Ownership, ACLs, and extended attributes stay exact-diff requirements. A focused test deletes a tracked file under `workspaces/`, `database/`, or `projects/`, backs up, restores onto a fresh prepared target, and shows the file absent. That reconciliation runs only on a target that passed the empty-target checks. No mode overwrites a populated primary.

**d. These four items are resolved only by OPS-01 implementation and its focused tests.** Registration does not declare them implemented or passed.

## U1 and U2 (recorded; no new authorization)

**U1.** Code preparation may proceed in parallel where the lane rules allow, during separately authorized implementation. Runtime tests that share LOCAL-RUNTIME run sequentially. Establish local capability before proposing cloud tests. Cloud tests require separate authorization, a stated spending limit, and a cleanup scope. No resources, implementation, or tests are authorized now. This does not authorize subagents.

**U2.** Stale execution or collaboration records require individual, recorded approval based on evidence. There is no automatic override, replay, or mutation. Changing a status does not establish that the work has stopped.

## RUNTIME-01 gateway continuation - 2026-10-07 - still pending

The first gateway failure was not only a missing database. All 13 smoke tests failed in `beforeAll`. The recorded assertion was `APP_BASE_URL is required for email auth`. TypeORM also logged `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:5432` because `NODE_ENV=test` resolves the host to localhost.

A task-owned PostgreSQL 15.17 container and Redis 7 container shared one network namespace with the Node 24 test container, so `127.0.0.1:5432` and `127.0.0.1:6379` were those fixtures. Credentials were test-only. `npm run migration:run` applied the gateway migrations after `NODE_PATH` exposed the workspace `ts-node`. Schema check found `api_keys`, `usage_records`, `billing_snapshots`, and `invoices`. Image identity remained `sha256:3d27e5c11e5786e309ec3e03f93ae536eb36e6e5eb3714d5eb3300a36157add0`. The three implementation-file hashes matched. `AI_PROVIDER=stub`. No provider call was made.

Final gateway command: `npm test -- --watchAll=false --ci`. Exit 1. Test Suites: 1 failed, 2 skipped, 170 passed, 171 of 173 total. Tests: 1 failed, 7 skipped, 2292 passed, 2300 total. The skipped suites remain the opt-in PostgreSQL suites. The failed test is `POST /api/ai/execute should execute with real provider`: expected 200, received 503. `GLOBAL_EXECUTION_ENABLED` defaults false and the guard returns 503 with `AI execution temporarily disabled for maintenance`. The current execute handler returns 202 `{ executionId, status: 'queued' }` and does not return provider output. Passing that test needs a gateway or test change outside the three-file scope, or a live provider execution this authorization does not allow.

Node 24 stays the selected runtime. The task stays ACTIVE and is not LANE-DONE. Occupancy was not changed, so the admission saturation proof was not regenerated.

Full gateway result: 7 skipped, 2294 passed, 2301 total, exit 0. Lane 1 is LANE-DONE and not LOCKED.

## OPS-01 follow-up — dump session, kv_set, and migration lifecycle — 2026-10-08

The residual review found that splitting a PostgreSQL 15 roles dump across psql processes breaks `\restrict` / `\unrestrict`, that `kv_set` can drop unrelated settings when `grep` fails, and that the migration ledger was not created by first-start, backup, or an interrupted migrate. Those paths are now connected under `ops/clean-baseline/`.

Role restore feeds one psql session, keeps the dump's meta-commands, and tolerates only `duplicate_object` on `CREATE ROLE`. `kv_set` leaves the original file in place when filtering returns an error. First-start and deploy stage attempted blobs before `start_migrate` and commit them only after verification. A from-maintenance recovery promotes that attempt when the database already has the rows. Backup copies the ledger when `DEPLOYED` exists, and restore installs it before writers start. A query error or a malformed timestamp is not treated as an empty migration set.

Shell regressions use a sanitized `pg_dumpall --roles-only` fixture and the first-start, interruption, backup, and restore function order. They passed. They do not execute entry points. Operational acceptance remains pending.

Prepare refuses before download when `caddy.service` or `caddy-api.service` is not already disabled or absent, or when either unit is active. If the copy or the restore cannot be completed, prepare stops and does not enable or start Caddy. The Caddyfile, `caddy hash-password`, and `caddy validate` are unchanged. This step does not start or reload Caddy. `caddy-api.service` is left disabled and inactive, and its packaged command is unchanged.

## OPS-01 Caddy mask-first installation — 2026-10-11

Keith authorized this slice only. `aisandbox-prepare` masks `caddy.service` and `caddy-api.service` with persistent `systemctl mask` before `dpkg`, and it does not create or modify `policy-rc.d`. The 2026-10-10 temporary policy-rc.d wrapper is withdrawn by this section. The pin, digest, cleanup-status behavior, Caddyfile, drop-in without `--environ`, and `aisandbox-prepare <sha>` command stay as recorded on 2026-10-10. `PREPARED` gains `caddy=masked` only after the final masked check. Enabling or starting Caddy remains a separately authorized pending step. `caddy-api.service` stays masked.

The wider service-start design and the operational acceptance matrix remain pending. This section does not adopt `--site`, Caddy activation, operational fault cases, restore-test repairs, `aisandbox.target` membership, or worker-readiness changes.

Reviewed control, not yet a host result: the pinned 2.11.7 postinst calls `deb-systemd-helper unmask` and then `enable`/`start`/`try-restart` with `|| true`. Ubuntu noble `init-system-helpers` 1.66ubuntu1 `unmask_service` leaves an administrator `/dev/null` mask in place unless `/var/lib/systemd/deb-systemd-helper-masked/<unit>` exists, and `deb-systemd-invoke` does not start a unit whose `is-enabled` output does not contain `enabled`. systemd 255 prints `not-found` and exits 4 for a missing unit file, prints `masked` and exits 1 for an administrator mask, prints `inactive` and exits 4 when `is-active` finds no unit file, and prints `inactive` and exits 3 when the unit is known but not active. A real `dpkg` install, mask of a not-yet-installed unit, and reboot on Ubuntu 24.04 remain unverified. Mocked checks are not operational acceptance. `aisb-ops-base-02` stays contaminated and is not contacted.

Admissible states, for both units together: Fresh means the package is absent, `not-installed`, or `config-files`, `caddy` is not on `PATH`, both units are `not-found`, and no mask link, wants link, or helper-owned mask record exists. Retry-before-dpkg means the package is absent or `not-installed`, and both units are already masked, inactive, and linked to `/dev/null`, with no wants link and no helper-owned mask record. Retry-after-dpkg means the package is `installed 2.11.7`, `caddy` is on `PATH`, and the masked condition already holds; prepare does not download or install again. A known state outside those three, a transitional package status, a version other than 2.11.7, or a probe whose output, exit code, and stderr do not match is refused with status 30 before download. Fresh and retry-before mask both units and require the masked condition before `dpkg` and again after `dpkg` returns 0. Download, checksum, and `dpkg` failures keep their own status.

The combined incremental spending limit stays US$15. The deletion deadline stays 2026-10-11 18:18:33 HKT. Neither is reset. Cleanup is not automated. ## OPS-01 aisandbox.target membership — 2026-10-11

Keith authorized this slice only. `aisandbox.target` is the application group. It is an operator handle, not a readiness signal. `aisandbox-gateway`, `aisandbox-ai`, `aisandbox-cm`, and `aisandbox-frontend` gain `PartOf=aisandbox.target` and `WantedBy=aisandbox.target`. Their existing dependencies, `AssertPathExists=!/var/lib/aisandbox-ops/maintenance`, and restart settings stay. `aisandbox-watchdog` gains the same membership and stays disabled unless AUTH-W is recorded. Caddy, `aisandbox-migrate`, `aisandbox-backup.service`, and `aisandbox-backup.timer` are not members. `PartOf=` does not start a member. Startup membership is the enablement link created by `systemctl enable`.

`aisandbox-prepare` installs the target and disables the target, the four writers, the watchdog, and the backup timer. It does not enable or start the target. `aisandbox-first-start` and `aisandbox-restore` enable `aisandbox.target` and then the four writers at the existing enablement step. They then remove the marker, start those writers one service at a time, and run the existing health check. They do not enable the watchdog. No entry point stops `aisandbox.target` or treats `systemctl is-active aisandbox.target` as readiness. The Caddy mask-first implementation stays as recorded. `--site`, Caddy activation, operational fault cases, restore-test repairs, and worker-readiness remain pending.

## OPS-01 ACTIVATION prerequisite — 2026-10-11

Keith authorized this slice only. `aisandbox-first-start` and `aisandbox-restore` require `/etc/aisandbox/ACTIVATION` before they take the operations lock, admit an operation, or change markers, application data, or services. ## OPS-01 worker readiness — 2026-10-11

Keith authorized this slice only. `health_wait` no longer treats the unconditional `Worker connected to ai-execution queue` journal line as AI readiness. Readiness still requires the existing HTTP checks inside the existing 120-second deadline and still returns 21 on timeout. AI readiness additionally requires `aisandbox-ai` to be active with a nonzero MainPID, a Redis client whose name field is exactly `bull:YWktZXhlY3V0aW9u`, an established socket for that client address owned by that MainPID, and a positive `TTL` on `bull:ai-execution:stalled-check`. A MainPID change during the observation is not accepted; the probe retries. Missing, malformed, failed, or mismatched observations do not pass. Redis credentials and raw client output are not logged. Socket acceptance compares parsed fields: the local endpoint must equal the selected client's `127.0.0.1` port, the peer must equal `127.0.0.1:6379`, and the owning PID must equal that MainPID. Malformed, wrong-peer, and reversed records do not pass. Each probe and the retry sleep are limited to the time remaining in the 120-second deadline. A result at or after that deadline is exit 21, not success. This matches installed BullMQ 5.70.1 and the nested ioredis 5.9.3 it loads: the unnamed `ai-execution` worker sets that client name, QueueEvents uses the `:qe` suffix, and the stall script sets the key with a millisecond expiry. No application source changes. The Caddy mask-first implementation, `aisandbox.target` membership, and ACTIVATION prerequisite stay as recorded. Host worker readiness is not verified. Other service-start slices remain pending.

The combined incremental spending limit stays US$15. The deletion deadline stays 2026-10-11 18:18:33 HKT. Neither is reset. Cleanup is not automated. ## OPS-01 t3.5 and fault-cleanup correction — 2026-10-15

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-15. It follows a reviewer reading of the frozen source before the next evidence group; no case failed. Defect 1: in the t3.5 branch of tests/operational/t3-quiesce.sh, the injected Q6 fault pushes ops-test-q6 into bull:ai-execution:wait (lib/ops-lib.sh:1661 to :1665) and aisandbox-backup exits 11 with phase=work-detected; the harness then called aisandbox-maint exit before deleting that item, but on phase work-detected maint exit takes a fresh sample (bin/aisandbox-maint:60 to :84), which would be ACTIVE and exit 11, so the case would fail. Defect 2: the t2.4 and t3.5 branches left their FAULT line in /etc/aisandbox/ops-test-mode, and neither harness defines a case cleanup, so the next case would start with an injected fault armed. Change: t3.5 now runs aisandbox-maint exit once and requires exit 11, phase=work-detected and aisandbox-ai not active; it then deletes the test item, calls clear_injected_fault, runs aisandbox-maint exit again and requires the marker absent. t2.4 now ends with clear_injected_fault. No other line in either harness changed. tests/operational/t3-quiesce.sh SHA-256 was 06cbc6c2d91fe8e96974f35f0ee56790ba8f8ec1f279b7fa6a32e73d4004ef9e and is 1ffdf9be7578314c58035cac271da725c39ba14a7632b007b044ab1249a85bc7. tests/operational/t2-lock-and-fault.sh SHA-256 was fee6e0d64551ad65144d5f6b7c5bd1c47b63adab9a35280abd8bc7df0491020a and is e0a3caaea4ddd88f5b8749ccde2b486359ab16b00fdaedb925a4e3a0af1f1f7c. New static test tests/static/t-fault-cleanup.sh, 1466 bytes, SHA-256 88a2050b2bc6ef6db4f7e280c6a8e5abf1b5273dbebb305374c1278837046de6, requires in t3.5 the order refused maint exit, DEL, clear_injected_fault, maint exit, and requires t2.4 to end with clear_injected_fault. Session report: the static test failed against the unchanged harness with "t3.5 must call aisandbox-maint exit twice" and passed after the change; bash -n passed on the three files; all eight static scripts exited 0. ## OPS-01 journal classifier correction — 2026-10-16

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-16. It follows the base-13 rollback stop. Defect: journal_has_writer_start (lib/ops-lib.sh:1432) counted any writer-journal line after the operation's journal cursor that matched "Starting |Started |Failed to start" as a writer start. With the maintenance marker present, systemd refuses each writer at boot because of AssertPathExists=!/var/lib/aisandbox-ops/maintenance and logs "Starting requested but asserts failed."; that line matched, so classify_failure returned post-start for a pre-start failure and aisandbox-deploy --from-maintenance refused with exit 30. Change: inside journal_has_writer_start only, the local list gains line, and the single grep check became a loop over the journal lines that skips any line containing "Starting requested but asserts failed." and returns 0 on any line containing "Starting ", "Started " or "Failed to start". The loop uses shell pattern matching without a pipe. The journalctl call, the return codes 0, 1 and 2, and classify_failure are unchanged. No other line in the library changed. lib/ops-lib.sh SHA-256 was 6804e14f53f68f50139ee81f08eeac635d4cad7f081aafb61fafd3ce0224db27 and is 43353f03af8dd5cee1287a9cbe0ae0dcf2008a3c64908bdf3b023fce2010eb10. New static test tests/static/t-journal-writer-start.sh, 1577 bytes, SHA-256 1cecbc102ff2b25077d79e67baf3de4fa9a1a71aa71673e87a720ff609172c4a, runs only that function with stubbed op_field and journalctl and checks eight cases: refusal lines only return 1; a refusal followed by a real Started line returns 0; a Starting line returns 0; a Failed to start line returns 0; stop lines only return 1; an empty journal returns 1; no cursor returns 2; a journalctl failure returns 2. Session report: the static test failed against the unchanged library with "assert refusals only: want 1 got 0" and passed after the change; bash -n passed on both files; all nine static scripts and all eleven regression scripts exited 0. The reviewer verified the diff and the two hashes from the files, reran bash -n, the nine static scripts and the eleven regression scripts, each exit 0, and had run the same eleven regression scripts on the unchanged tree before the change, each exit 0. classify_failure is used only by the failure traps of prepare, first-start, restore and deploy, by aisandbox-maint status, and by aisandbox-deploy --from-maintenance. The change alters a result only when the journal after the cursor holds the systemd refusal line, which appears only when a writer is refused at boot with the marker present; no accepted case depends on such a classification. ## OPS-01 restore-case harness correction — 2026-10-18

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-18. It follows a reviewer reading of the restore-case harnesses before the T1.5, T4 and correction c group; no case failed. Defect 1: tests/operational/t4c-deleted-tracked.sh deleted database/tracked-delete-me.sql and required that path to be tracked in /opt/aisandbox, but it is tracked only in the fixture repository that tests/fixture-builder.sh:75 builds and not at the application target SHA 36f9a213b7e16c0cb12587a2d74cd0f679fdc54c, so t4c-source would fail at its first check. Defect 2: the t1.5-sha branch of tests/operational/t1-prepare.sh overwrote /etc/aisandbox/PREPARED with a zero SHA and never put it back, so no later restore case could use that target. Change: t4c now deletes database/README.md, which is tracked at the application target SHA and is not a migration file; no other line in that harness changed. t1.5-sha now saves PREPARED with cp -p before planting the zero SHA, puts it back with cp -p right after the refused restore, checks it with cmp, removes the saved copy, keeps its rc=10 and no-DEPLOYED assertions, and ends with require_prepared_idle; no other branch changed. tests/fixture-builder.sh, lib/ and bin/ are unchanged. tests/operational/t4c-deleted-tracked.sh SHA-256 was 0da2e858128ce1cb49542f83d89e15526440d0c7ac1c49112a1a2cddf350347c and is 9c3a3faf45a0ebf6f7ddc288b093b8bd19f851f03ddbe4fcc785f382840ed9e1. tests/operational/t1-prepare.sh SHA-256 was 38ed93e2e3f48805473b8aea6f8ea6515702ca86b4ae248d868f2555e2d532bd and is c3ec35b39b760074e121f458b72df8264fff8afb89d0c814d4e9de95c2ca2775. ## OPS-01 t4.2-uid harness correction authorization — 2026-10-21

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-21, before any code change. t4.2-uid has not run on a guest. Defect: the t4.2-uid branch of tests/operational/t4-metadata.sh, lines 201 to 207, runs usermod -u 7301 aisandbox and only then calls refuse_with_snapshot, whose before snapshot reads the checkout with git; the 2026-10-19 pilot harness correction record states that git refuses the checkout for the aisandbox user after the uid change, so the case would fail before the restore runs. Its after snapshot is also taken before usermod -u 7300 puts the uid back. Change, authorized and not yet implemented: the branch keeps require_prepared_idle first, takes prepare_snapshot "$evidence.before" before usermod -u 7301, runs /usr/local/sbin/aisandbox-restore once with the bundle and key and requires exit 10, puts the uid back with usermod -u 7300, then takes prepare_snapshot "$evidence.after" and runs assert_restore_wrote_nothing on the two snapshots. No other branch of that harness and no file under lib/ or bin/ changes. A static check added to tests/static/t-restore-arrangement.sh, reading source only, requires that order in the t4.2-uid branch. Recorded plainly: with aisandbox at uid 7301 the refusal is expected at the empty-target check, bin/aisandbox-restore:62, whose git status of the checkout (lib/ops-lib.sh:1297, through git_as_owner at lib/ops-lib.sh:84) fails for the same reason, before the manifest uid/gid check at bin/aisandbox-restore:85 is reached. So t4.2-uid can show that restore refuses and writes nothing when the aisandbox uid has changed; it cannot show that the manifest uid check is the step that refuses. Keith also decided on 2026-10-21, for a later sheet and authorization, how the remaining restore group runs: one source and three targets. On target T-B: t1.5-unprepared, t1.5-sha, t1.5-redis, the T4.2 refusals, then t1.5. On T-C: t4c-source on the source, then t4c-target. On T-D: t4.3. Each target generates its own throwaway age key, and only its public recipient leaves it. The source makes one backup per target: t4c-source for T-C, and a plain aisandbox-backup step for T-B and for T-D. The bundles move through Cursor's machine under Keith's 2026-10-18 exception. This record authorizes none of that. A new frozen package, new preparation files and a new authorization on fresh guests are required before t4.2-uid runs; this record does not build them. This authorization is not T4.2 evidence. Acceptance status is unchanged. No disposable guest or operational run is authorized. OPS-01 stays ACTIVE. Not LANE-DONE. Not LOCKED. ## OPS-01 T2 guest-local migration commit authorization — 2026-10-22

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-22, before any code change. Blocker: t2.2, t2.4-deploy and t2.5-source need a target commit that adds an executable migration. t2.2 requires one (tests/operational/t2-lock-and-fault.sh:41 to 42), and bin/aisandbox-deploy runs its pre-migration backup and the migrate unit only when count_added_executable_migrations is above zero (:164 to 165 and :186), so neither fault case reaches its fault without one. No commit after the application target SHA 36f9a213b7e16c0cb12587a2d74cd0f679fdc54c adds a migration, and none is added to the application source for a test. Keith decided on 2026-10-22 to use a guest-local commit, made on each guest that needs it and never pushed. Change, authorized and not yet implemented: a new file tests/operational/t2-synthetic-migration.txt, 550 bytes, SHA-256 35204681f1744b9ae4fe195b471890ae0bb9b3785f78e252e1523b9b986df0cd, git blob 0b4412e10880285e1134d0069269de5f81d05763, holding the TypeORM migration class OpsTestMarker1799000000000, whose up() creates the table ops_test_marker if it does not exist and whose down() drops it; a new branch t2-commit in tests/operational/t2-lock-and-fault.sh; and a new static script tests/static/t-t2-commit.sh. The t2-commit branch takes no argument and, as the aisandbox user and with git plumbing only: refuses if refs/ops-test/t2-commit exists or HEAD already holds services/api-gateway/src/migrations/1799000000000-OpsTestMarker.ts; writes the fixture as a blob; builds a tree of HEAD plus that one file, mode 100644, in a temporary index inside a new temporary folder; creates a commit with HEAD as its only parent, author and committer "OPS-01 T2 <ops-test@aisandbox.invalid>" at 1792627200 +0000, and the message "ops-test: T2 synthetic migration"; stores it at refs/ops-test/t2-commit; and deletes the temporary folder. It then requires HEAD unchanged, no tracked difference from HEAD in the real index or working tree, exactly one A line, for that path, in git diff --name-status from HEAD to the commit, and count_added_executable_migrations equal to 1; it writes its evidence file with host, parent, commit, tree, blob and path, and prints the commit. It never runs git commit, checkout, reset, merge or push. The static script, reading source only, requires the fixture's SHA-256, the branch's commit-tree and update-ref calls and its HEAD check after them, and no git commit, checkout, reset, merge or push in the branch. No other harness branch and no file under lib/ or bin/ changes. From the repository objects the reviewer computed that, at parent 36f9a213b7e16c0cb12587a2d74cd0f679fdc54c, the commit is 641beea159e08fdfc14abda47927cc5f7beda8ae with tree b266398a0702bd013b6f84abf23ba579af565de0 on any guest; this has not been observed on a guest. The gateway build (tsc over src/**/*) compiles the file into dist/src/migrations, which the migrate unit loads through dist/data-source.js. Planned order, for a later sheet and authorization and not authorized here: one fresh source and one fresh target. Last, t2.5-source-recheck on the source. The capture helper needs a T2 slice that runs t2-commit, checks its evidence against the commit above and passes the commit to those cases; this record does not build it. Recorded plainly: the synthetic migration adds one empty table, so these cases can show how deploy, its pre-migration backup, the migrate unit, the fault handling and restore behave around an added migration; they cannot show that any real application migration applies. The commit exists only on the guests, not on GitHub. ## OPS-01 prepare_snapshot harness correction authorization — 2026-10-23

Keith authorized this correction only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-23, before any code change. Defect: prepare_snapshot in tests/lib/case-helpers.sh lists both snapshot-store folders with one command at line 208, find /opt/aisandbox/services/snapshot-store /opt/aisandbox/snapshot-store -mindepth 1 -print 2>/dev/null piped into sort. find exits 1 when either folder is missing, so under set -euo pipefail a call made without || return 1 ends the harness. Those calls are tests/operational/t1-prepare.sh:21 and :28 (refuse_case), :140 and :145 (t1.5-unprepared), and tests/operational/t4-metadata.sh:219 and :226 (t4.2-uid). On T-B on 2026-10-23 both folders were absent and t1.5-unprepared ended at t1-prepare.sh:140 before PREPARED was put back, as recorded in the restore-group run closure record, repository commit b7ccaff71d428206d34fae3ff5263d34993618eb. Recorded plainly: earlier passing cases ran where both folders existed, and the fixture builder creates both (tests/fixture-builder.sh:33); the 2>/dev/null also hid any other find error; and tests/regression/t4-snapshot-files.sh replaces find with a stub that always succeeds and runs without set -e or pipefail, so it could not catch this. Change, authorized and not yet implemented, in prepare_snapshot only: add the locals store and stores, and replace line 208 with a loop over a folder list, which is AISB_SNAPSHOT_STORES (one path per line, for tests only) or, when that is unset or empty, /opt/aisandbox/services/snapshot-store then /opt/aisandbox/snapshot-store. For a path that exists or is a symbolic link, the loop runs find on that path with -mindepth 1 -print piped into sort, and prepare_snapshot returns 1 if that fails; for a missing path it prints absent=<path>. Entries are listed in the same order as before; a missing folder is now recorded in the snapshot instead of failing it, and any find error now fails the snapshot and is not hidden. No call site changes. tests/regression/t4-snapshot-files.sh: the find stub is removed and AISB_SNAPSHOT_STORES points the three existing cases at their temporary folder, so they use the real find; three cases are added, each calling prepare_snapshot without || under set -euo pipefail in a subshell: both folders absent passes and prints both absent lines in order; a present folder is listed while the other is recorded absent; and a find that fails makes the call fail. New file tests/static/t-snapshot-stores.sh, reading source only, requires that prepare_snapshot no longer contains the single find of both folders or 2>/dev/null piped into sort, and that it contains, exactly once each and in this order, the folder-list line, the read loop, the existence test, the find with || return 1, the absent line and the end of the loop. Reviewer prototype, off-guest on a scratch copy: the new regression fails its absent and present cases against the current helper and passes all nine cases against the changed one; the static check fails against the current helper and against three altered versions (|| true in place of || return 1, no absent line, and 2>/dev/null kept); bash -n passes on the changed helper; every static and regression script exits 0 on the changed copy. No file under lib/ or bin/, no operational harness, no call site and no other function changes. Earlier accepted evidence is not changed by this record. The frozen package CLEAN-BASELINE-OPS-01-TRANSFER-2026-10-22B.tar.gz, SHA-256 229af19d0df03c409d8e62bff603871060016acdabd3c9390315e2bc341b84c7, built from repository commit 73956057f3e7bfa024af5feaaa0ee31883051dd3, contains the uncorrected helper and must not be used for a new T-B window. A new frozen package, new preparation files and a new authorization on fresh guests, a source and a T-B with at most two guests beside aisandbox-staging, are required before any T-B case runs again; this record does not build or authorize them. This authorization is not T1.5 or T4.2 evidence. Acceptance status is unchanged. No disposable guest or operational run is authorized. OPS-01 stays ACTIVE. Not LANE-DONE. Not LOCKED. DEPLOY-01 stays unadmitted. HOST_CLEAN=NO.

The occupancy block, sidecar, and both proof files stay byte-for-byte unchanged, so saturation validation is not rerun. GOVERNANCE is UNOWNED.

## OPS-01 t4.2-redis cleanup correction and t4.2-noacl amendment authorization — 2026-10-25

Keith authorized this correction and amendment only, under ops/clean-baseline/, on 2026-10-25, before any code change. Defect 1, t4.2-redis: tests/operational/t4-metadata.sh:207 to :216 plants the Redis key ops-test, runs refuse_with_snapshot, and then deletes the key with redis-cli DEL; refuse_with_snapshot calls prepare_snapshot, which ends with unset REDISCLI_AUTH, so the DEL runs without the Redis password, and redis-cli exits 0 on the error reply, so the key stays and the case still passes. On aisb-ops-base-27 on 2026-10-23 the case-evidence snapshots showed redis=0 through t4.2-role and redis=1 from t4.2-redis onward, and t1.5 was later refused with empty-target failed path=redis entry=DBSIZE, as recorded in the T-B window closure record, repository commit b81514f4a5ddc1a9dc5a1310691377b996d88bd5. Change 1, authorized and not yet implemented, in that branch only: after unset AISB_SNAPSHOT_REDIS_KEYS, call redis_auth_env again, require that redis-cli DEL ops-test prints 1 and that redis-cli EXISTS ops-test then prints 0, and then unset REDISCLI_AUTH; the bare DEL is removed. t1.5-redis and t1.2-redis are not affected and do not change. Defect 2, t4.2-noacl: tests/operational/t4-metadata.sh:231 to :273 mounts its fixture image with -o loop,noacl under set -e; on kernel 7.0.0-1012-aws, which logs "ext4: Unknown parameter 'noacl'", the mount fails with exit 32 before any restore, after the fixture image of the whole tree has been built, as recorded on 2026-10-08 in the work-folder noacl fixture review proposal and on 2026-10-23 on aisb-ops-base-27. Amendment, by Keith decision of 2026-10-25, adopting that 2026-10-08 proposal: on a kernel whose log shows "Unknown parameter 'noacl'" after a noacl mount attempt, t4.2-noacl is FIXTURE_UNSUPPORTED and is not accepted on that kernel; the restore rule is unchanged, and a failed mount, a non-ext4 mount or a mocked findmnt does not satisfy t4.2-noacl. Change 2, authorized and not yet implemented, in that branch only: before measuring or copying the tree, create a 16 MiB probe image /var/tmp/aisb-ops-fixtures/noacl-probe.img, make an ext4 file system on it, and try mount -o loop,noacl onto /mnt/aisb-noacl-probe, writing its stderr to the case evidence; remove the probe image and folder in every outcome, with the case cleanup also removing them; if the probe mount fails and the last 50 kernel log lines contain "Unknown parameter 'noacl'", print and record FIXTURE_UNSUPPORTED and exit 77 without calling aisandbox-restore; if the probe mount fails otherwise, exit with its status; if it succeeds, the existing fixture and restore assertions run unchanged as the only acceptance path. Exit 77 is not a pass, and like any non-zero case exit it marks the host contaminated, so a sheet runs t4.2-noacl last or not at all. New file tests/static/t-redis-noacl.sh, reading source only, requires in the t4.2-redis branch the second redis_auth_env between the unset of AISB_SNAPSHOT_REDIS_KEYS and the checked DEL, the checked DEL and EXISTS lines once each in order, and no unchecked DEL; and in the t4.2-noacl branch the probe mount, the kernel-message test and exit 77 before the tree is measured, then the two existing fixture mounts, and FIXTURE_UNSUPPORTED once. Reviewer prototype, off-guest on a scratch copy: tests/operational/t4-metadata.sh goes from 11590 bytes, SHA-256 09878cb97d1af192df36371b80290d9cd13e4d2b9031b7c027599e5d2f5c54e7, to 12591 bytes, SHA-256 d7cf1ebb3fb2fd33084055730737409804b98c5fea20f117de9b1f79991f4169, and the static file is 2222 bytes, SHA-256 0f866015553e8de7d69b95dfd52878caf72e1453d2a0975bb467e4f10915b86d. With stubs, the t4.2-redis branch of the current harness passed while leaving the key, the changed branch removed it, and the changed branch failed when the DEL did not remove the key; the t4.2-noacl branch exited 77 with FIXTURE_UNSUPPORTED and no restore call when the kernel rejected noacl, exited 32 on another mount failure, and ran the existing restore path when the mount succeeded. The static check passes against the changed harness and fails against the current harness and against three altered versions (no second redis_auth_env, an unchecked DEL, and exit 0 in place of exit 77); bash -n passes on all 43 shell files; all fourteen static scripts and all eleven regression scripts exit 0 on the changed copy. No other branch, no file under lib/ or bin/, and no other harness changes. Earlier accepted evidence is not changed. t4.2-redis must be rerun after this correction; t1.5 still needs a fresh T-B; both need a new frozen package and a new authorization on fresh guests, which this record does not build or authorize. This authorization is not T1.5 or T4.2 evidence. Acceptance status is unchanged. No disposable guest or operational run is authorized. OPS-01 stays ACTIVE. Not LANE-DONE. Not LOCKED. DEPLOY-01 stays unadmitted. HOST_CLEAN=NO.

The occupancy block, sidecar, and both proof files stay byte-for-byte unchanged, so saturation validation is not rerun. GOVERNANCE is UNOWNED.

## OPS-01 t4.2-redis cleanup correction and t4.2-noacl amendment result — 2026-10-25

Recorded plainly: neither changed branch has run on a guest. The frozen package CLEAN-BASELINE-OPS-01-TRANSFER-2026-10-23.tar.gz contains the uncorrected harness and must not be used for the next T-B window; t4.2-redis must be rerun and t1.5 run on a fresh T-B, which needs a new frozen package, new preparation files and a new authorization on fresh guests; this record does not build or authorize them. This correction is not T1.5 or T4.2 evidence. Acceptance status is unchanged. No disposable guest or operational run is authorized. OPS-01 stays ACTIVE. Not LANE-DONE. Not LOCKED. ## OPS-01 short T-B window run authorization (base-28 and base-29) — 2026-10-25

This runs the two T-B steps the T-B window closed in repository commit b81514f4a5ddc1a9dc5a1310691377b996d88bd5 left open, t4.2-redis and t1.5, after the t4.2-redis cleanup correction and t4.2-noacl amendment in repository commit 11fd9ecb907e109069cd96e0a12e9c10f2215a8b. Keith approved on 2026-10-25 two fresh Lightsail instances: aisb-ops-base-28, the source, and aisb-ops-base-29, target T-B; each in account 002559670476, ap-southeast-1, Ubuntu 24.04, medium_3_0 (2 vCPU, 4 GB RAM, 80 GB SSD). For each, remove HTTP TCP 80 in the create wizard if it offers that; otherwise remove that one rule once after creation, before the first SSH connection and before any setup, and record which happened. Each firewall is SSH TCP 22 only before any setup, with no other firewall change. The maximum incremental cost is US$8 for each guest and its associated usage, US$16 in total. Delete each guest by its own AWS createdAt plus 72 hours, or earlier to remain within that budget. At most two OPS-01 guests run beside aisandbox-staging. The approved scope is the two sheets only, in the cross-guest order of section 1 of the source sheet. On each guest: stage and prereq, t1.1 run detached as setup, and ACTIVATION created by hand and bound to that guest recorded machine id and the label aisb-ops-base-28-run or aisb-ops-base-29-run. On the source only: t1.6 first-start run detached as setup with the confirmation line confirm; then, after writing the T-B public recipient to /etc/aisandbox/age-recipient.txt, plain-backup-tb through /root/aisb-run-capture.sh, which runs aisandbox-backup once, stopping and restarting the writers and writing one bundle encrypted to that recipient. On T-B only: one throwaway age key generated on T-B and kept there at /root/aisb-age/target.key, mode 0600, with only its public recipient printed. Under the Keith 2026-10-18 exception, only the T-B bundle, that bundle .identity file and the evidence file plain-backup-tb are copied from the source to T-B through the Cursor machine, outside the repository, with the SHA-256 checked at every hop, and the local copies are deleted after both exports. On T-B, in order and each through the helper with the T-B key: t4.2-redis, which must be refused with exit 10, leave the prepared target unchanged and leave Redis without the planted key, then t1.5, which restores the bundle with one newline on stdin, writes DEPLOYED and starts the writers. Then export from each guest only captures whose secret scan completed with no match, and delete both guests. If a step fails on a guest, that guest sequence stops, and so does every later step that depends on it; nothing is repaired; the other guest exports and is deleted as usual. t1.1 and t1.6 on the source and t1.1 on T-B are setup and claim nothing new. No t1.6 on T-B, t1.6-repeat, any other T1.5 or T4.2 case including t4.2-noacl, plain-backup-td, t4c-source, source ACL step, t4c-target, t4.3, t4.1-source, t4.1-target, t1.5-repeat, t2-commit, any T2 or T3 case, or tb-post-start-reboot. No reboot, second commit or deploy; no backup other than plain-backup-tb; no restore other than those the T-B sheet names. No private key leaves T-B. A stop raised by a readiness check (a Block A, 5.0, 5.0a or 5.0c block), with no failed step, capture, manifest or scan, is reported to the reviewer, who may direct that the unstarted steps continue on the same guests; the record states that plainly. Each block is delivered as a file, copied to the guest and checked by SHA-256 there before it runs, never piped into ssh; every connection uses the recorded key and known-hosts file with StrictHostKeyChecking=yes, IPQoS=throughput and ConnectTimeout=30, one at a time. On 2026-10-26, after the slice B result in repository commit b9cba67aa08156450b6fd144f93dc705c367fe04, Keith approved the redirect fix and chose to carry it inside DEPLOY-01 (route A). A separate task was not possible beside DEPLOY-01: a scratch pre-run of the unchanged validator on that alternative failed at its pair check (exit 2), because its evidence-class rule denies every pairing with a STAGING-RUNTIME lane. Change (exact): in caddy/Caddyfile the two site-level lines redir / /en 307 and redir /app /en/app 307 become a handle / block holding redir * /en 307 and a handle /app block holding redir * /en/app 307, so Caddy runs basic_auth before them; the site address, basic_auth, the /api/* handler and the default handler are unchanged (new file 253 bytes, SHA-256 2b77968c2029fd2b8abb6a2d20de7a0cc594765cc33f321d0b1414f6aebad2c8). tests/static/t-caddy-site.sh gains one check, public-redirects-behind-basic-auth: basic_auth at site level, no site-level redir, and exactly those two redirects inside handle / and handle /app (new file 4996 bytes, SHA-256 6c7b926363f251545aafd444fb4ed9a543ebc597e6beccf34fcd6fd08a790afc). ## CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01 and CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-02 registration for the C1 check 9 defect — 2026-10-27

On 2026-10-27, after DEPLOY-01 returned to READY (repository commits baec5056ad60eecccad4744d23f2dc91555acf67 and 9ef0045f8094ed3e5dce5c8b68adec969c6b865b), Keith approved two tasks for the C1 check 9 defect, chose to fix both of its parts before the C1 repeat, and approved their scopes. The defect, verified in the source on 2026-10-27: handleConfirmCheckpointRevert in frontend/app/[locale]/app/page.tsx reverts, then reloads the checkpoint list, the files and the preview, but saves no project version, and its preview refresh returns before its own autosave when the preview is already running; project autosave has a 60-second minimum interval (AUTOSAVE_MIN_INTERVAL_MS in frontend/lib/autosave-rate-limit.ts), and an autosave skipped by that limit is dropped, never retried; and project snapshots exclude .git by design (PROJ-02-03) while checkpoints are per session (ARCHITECTURE.md section 11.5), so a new session of the project starts with no checkpoint history. Opening a project after its container is gone starts a fresh session restored from the latest project snapshot, so a revert, or a save the limit skipped, is lost without warning. Light authority check for this named work: PRD.md section 3.A lists, as a CURRENT project capability, that files, conversation history and checkpoints survive session end; ARCHITECTURE.md section 11.5 describes per-session checkpoints as CURRENT; the backlog holds no task for either gap (PROJ-03-C2b, PROJ-03-C2e and PROJ-03-C2f added the preview, AI and file-save autosave triggers, none for a revert). CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01 (fix A) makes the project copy keep the newest workspace state. After a successful checkpoint revert, the frontend saves a project autosave snapshot at once with a new source revert, not subject to the 60-second limit. A file-save, preview or AI autosave skipped by the limit schedules one trailing autosave of the then-current workspace when the interval has passed; a newer request replaces it, and a change of the selected session or project cancels it. The new source has a label in en.json, zh-TW.json and zh-CN.json, and a failed post-revert autosave shows a translated warning that the revert is not yet saved to the project. Write scope, exactly: frontend/app/[locale]/app/page.tsx, frontend/lib/project-autosave.ts, frontend/lib/project-autosave.test.ts, frontend/components/workspace/workspace-snapshots.logic.ts, frontend/components/workspace/workspace-snapshots.logic.test.ts, frontend/messages/en.json, frontend/messages/zh-TW.json and frontend/messages/zh-CN.json. The machine writePaths list the other seven files, because the validator cannot encode the brackets in frontend/app/[locale]/app/page.tsx; that file is covered by FRONTEND, as for BUILDER-CREDIT-UX-01. Mutexes FRONTEND and I18N; no hot-file lease; no shared contract; workstream RELIABILITY; lifecycle 3-STEP (registration and admission, implementation, consolidation and LOCK); evidence class LOCAL-TESTS (npx tsc --noEmit, npm test and npm run build in frontend, with new tests for both paths); start condition READY; depends on nothing; saturationClass FORCING, because DEPLOY-01 waits for it; revert isolation acceptable, frontend only. Not in scope: any backend or container-manager change, the checkpoint and revert APIs, the snapshot format, the rate-limit value, and any other UI change. Its staging proof is the later DEPLOY-01 C1 repeat. CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-02 (fix B) makes checkpoint history survive into a new session of the same project: after a reopen, the earlier checkpoints are listed and a revert to one of them works. Lifecycle 4-STEP: Step 1 is this registration; Step 2, a stage-start record, chooses how the history is kept for each project and restored into a new session, updates ARCHITECTURE.md section 11.5 with that design, and freezes the exact write scope, mutexes and evidence class; Step 3 is implementation; Step 4 is consolidation and LOCK. It is registered with writeSetPrecision PROVISIONAL and admissionUncertain true, so it cannot be admitted before that stage-start, with the provisional write areas services/container-manager, services/api-gateway and frontend, the provisional mutexes CONTAINER-MANAGER, GATEWAY, FRONTEND, I18N and MIGRATION, and the provisional evidence class LOCAL-TESTS; it depends on CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01, because both write the frontend; start condition NOT_READY until the stage-start; saturationClass FORCING; workstream RELIABILITY. Sequence: Part 2 of this record adds both task bodies with their machine-registration stanzas and both sidecar candidates, admits CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01 to Lane 1 holding FRONTEND and I18N, and reruns the canonical validator. After A is LOCKED comes B's stage-start; after B is LOCKED, a record re-admits DEPLOY-01 to deploy the fix to ainow-staging-01 and repeat C1 checks 1 to 3, 6, 7, 4 and 9. Nothing else changes: DEPLOY-01 stays READY and not admitted with startCondition NOT_READY; ainow-staging-01 and the old host stay as recorded; this record authorizes no runtime, staging or provider action.

GOVERNANCE is held for this record. Part 2 adds the two task bodies and sidecar candidates, admits CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01 to Lane 1, updates the occupancy block and the board, releases GOVERNANCE UNOWNED and reruns the canonical validator.

Behaviour: after a successful checkpoint revert, while the request is still current, a project is selected and no project open is in progress, the page saves a project autosave snapshot with the new source revert and minIntervalMs 0; on success it records the autosave time, reloads the project versions and cancels any pending trailing save; on failure the revert stays reverted, any older project message is cleared, and the History & Controls project-action slot shows recovery.workspace.revertNotSavedToProject. On 2026-10-27, after CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-01 was locked (repository commit 22cd8d585495b7cb225a7d0b0256c7308ba70c02), Keith chose option 1 for CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-02: the container-manager keeps a git history bundle per project, saved after each recorded checkpoint and restored into a new session after its files are restored, and the gateway lists and reverts checkpoints across the project's sessions. ## CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-02 Step 3 result and Step 4 consolidation — 2026-10-27

CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-02 Step 3, implementation, is complete in two repository commits that change exactly the 16 files of its frozen write scope: fb060d1247c28d85a6e2d5b4d0deb9fc591aabc5, the container-manager side (5 files, 1000 lines added, 3 removed), and 70ea8b069d7d94657cd40431853d13c9f51fbbd3, the gateway side (11 files, 1049 lines added, 40 removed). Container-manager: each checkpoint commit gets the ref refs/checkpoints/<hash> before it is recorded, and a failure only logs; the new InternalCheckpointHistoryController, behind InternalServiceAuthGuard, serves POST /api/internal/checkpoint-history/:sessionId/save and /restore with the frozen responses; save bundles the session repository with git bundle create --all inside the container, copies it from the host workspace path to <projects root>/_checkpoint-history/<projectId>.bundle through a temporary file and a rename, and removes the in-container bundle; restore copies the stored bundle into the workspace, fetches +refs/*:refs/* with --update-head-ok, points HEAD at the saved branch (main, then master, when several match) or detaches it at the saved commit, runs git reset -q and removes the temporary bundle in every outcome; both ids must be UUIDs. Gateway: ContainerManagerHttpClient calls the two endpoints with X-Internal-Service-Key; after a checkpoint is recorded, the internal ledger endpoint starts one background step that looks up the session and saves the project history, with any failure only logged, so the record request is unaffected; the session snapshot restore endpoint restores the files, then awaits the history restore when the session has a project and logs a failure without failing the open; checkpoint listing returns the owner's ledger rows across all sessions of the project, newest first; revert and diff accept a row with the hash from the current session or another session of the same project and user, and sessions without a project keep their original check. Execution: Cursor CLI through the cursor-delegate workflow, model grok-4.7-xhigh (served as Grok 4.7 256K Extra High), in two runs of two passes each: run 1, session 8eae432b-21aa-40be-9ed6-674d02565ef3, plus a reviewer follow-up that removed a host-side delete of the container-owned bundle (on the host it would fail with a permission error on every save and log a false error); run 2, session e640842b-ed6e-4022-8bf7-c25ecf6a2ce1, plus a reviewer follow-up that moved the session lookup into the background step, as design item 10 requires. Cursor changed no file outside the scope. Local environment, before the gates: services/api-gateway/node_modules was missing, so the gateway resolved ioredis 5.10.0 instead of its pinned 5.9.3, its build failed and its test run crashed after 15 failing suites, all before any change; Keith approved npm ci at the repository root, which restored the lockfile install and changed no tracked file. The gateway suite then ran out of memory with the default Jest workers, so its gate runs with --maxWorkers=2; there, src/__tests__/smoke.integration.spec.ts fails its 14 tests in the baseline and after the change, because it needs a live PostgreSQL and Redis, which this task does not run. The gates use Node 22.14.0, the version the installed better-sqlite3 was built for. Reviewer verification on the committed trees: container-manager npm test passes 9 suites and 156 tests (137 before) and npm run build passes; gateway npx jest --maxWorkers=2 passes 171 suites and 2309 tests (170 and 2280 before), with only smoke.integration.spec.ts failing as in the baseline, and npm run build passes. The reopen criterion, earlier checkpoints listed and a revert to one of them working in a new session, is met at the API level by the tests; its live end-to-end proof, including the editor, file tree, preview and list refresh, is the CLEAN-BASELINE-DEPLOY-01 C1 repeat on staging, as the stage-start records. Not verified here: real Docker, git and database behaviour. Residual risks, recorded and not changed: bundle size grows with history and there is no pruning; restore installs git on demand and needs the container network; history is restored only through the session snapshot restore endpoint, so a project opened without any snapshot gets no history; a checkpoint made in a session whose save failed stays listed, and a revert to it fails as a revert does today. ## SESSION-STALE-01 Step 2 stage-start: end a session whose container is gone, and retry the open — 2026-10-27

On 2026-10-27, after CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-03 was locked (repository commit 3bc76713c823ba533fc2e24f8171f96e79d0f73b), the reviewer compared three designs for SESSION-STALE-01 from the source: reviving a stopped container, ending a dead session on its first use with a retried open, and a sweep when the container manager starts. Keith chose option 2. Design, frozen in docs/SESSION-STALE-01-STAGE-START.md: when one of the seven session operations in the container manager fails and the session container is missing or not running, it ends the session with termination_reason container_lost, logs the governance event, schedules the usual cleanup and returns 410; the gateway is unchanged, because its existing 410 reconciliation already ends the Postgres session with the reason from the message; the frontend retries a project open once in a fresh session when the open into a reused session returns 410. No shared contract changes and no visible text. Scope, exact: services/container-manager/src/sessions/sessions.service.ts and its spec, services/container-manager/src/docker/docker-runtime.service.ts and its spec, frontend/lib/open-project-in-fresh-session.ts and its test, and frontend/components/workspace/workspace-projects.logic.ts and its test; mutexes CONTAINER-MANAGER and FRONTEND; evidence class LOCAL-TESTS; gates npm test and npm run build in services/container-manager, and npx tsc --noEmit, npm test and npm run build in frontend. With the design Keith approved one change to the registered acceptance criteria: a dead session is ended the first time it is used, and a dead session nobody touches still counts until it expires. ARCHITECTURE.md section 4 gets a PLANNED note, which turns CURRENT at the lock. Not chosen: reviving containers (a larger behaviour change that still needs option 2 when a container is removed) and a startup sweep (it needs a change to the internal stop contract, can run before the gateway is up, and misses a container that dies while the services run). Follow-up candidate recorded, not registered: the leftover debug fetch to 127.0.0.1:7870 in startSessionContainer in the container manager.

GOVERNANCE is held for this record. Part 2 freezes the exact scope in the task body and sidecar, admits SESSION-STALE-01 to Lane 1 with CONTAINER-MANAGER and FRONTEND, updates the occupancy block and the board, releases GOVERNANCE UNOWNED and reruns the canonical validator.

## SESSION-STALE-01 Step 3 result and Step 4 consolidation — 2026-10-27

SESSION-STALE-01 Step 3, implementation, is complete in two repository commits that change exactly the eight files of its frozen write scope: b9b818f3e00563f92c6cf5d2b7bda7b7d04e5393 (run 1, container manager: sessions.service.ts 125 added and 37 removed, its spec 458 added, docker-runtime.service.ts 25 added, its spec 79 added) and c003daf64bb1a07d43759724e3f0b19fbfc0170b (run 2, frontend: open-project-in-fresh-session.ts 21 added and 1 removed, its test 156 added, workspace-projects.logic.ts 14 added and 1 removed, its test 50 added). Behaviour: DockerRuntimeService.getSessionContainerState returns running, stopped or missing and lets Docker errors through. In SessionsService the seven session operations call terminateIfContainerLost only when their Docker call fails: when the container is stopped or missing it applies the guarded termination with container_lost, logs the governance event, schedules the existing cleanup when the container is stopped, clears the in-memory tracking and throws GoneException with (reason: container_lost); when the container is running, or the state check fails, the original error is rethrown. No gateway notification is sent; the gateway ends its own session from the 410. In the frontend, openWorkspaceProject throws ProjectOpenError carrying the HTTP status with the same message, and openProjectInFreshSession retries the open once in a fresh session when the open into a reused session fails with 410. Refinement within the frozen design, recorded here: the container cleanup is scheduled only for a stopped container, because a missing container has nothing to stop or remove and its cleanup would only log an error; the stage-start required tests named the scheduled cleanup for both cases. Execution: Cursor CLI through the cursor-delegate workflow in two runs: run 1 with model grok-4.7-xhigh (served as Grok 4.7 256K Extra High), session 4f842d5a-6903-489b-b47e-2f7b8ddfcb37; run 2 with model grok-4.7-high (served as Grok 4.7 256K High), session edf8db9c-490c-4280-a810-cf6b824054d0. On 2026-10-27, after CHECKPOINT-PERSIST-03 and SESSION-STALE-01 were both COMPLETE AND LOCKED (repository commits 3bc76713c823ba533fc2e24f8171f96e79d0f73b and 035040d41fd552a5d278e43647e65120ee61995c), Keith approved AUTH-5c: CLEAN-BASELINE-DEPLOY-01 is re-admitted to Lane 1, the fixed code is deployed to ainow-staging-01, and C1 checks 1 to 3, 4 and 9 are repeated on the existing C1 repeat project. Browser steps are Keith's; host steps come from Cursor desktop; records run through Cursor CLI; evidence goes under C:\Users\knlee\aisb-preflight\CLEAN-BASELINE-EVIDENCE-20261027\C1S\. Failure rule: any failed step stops the run with no repair or retry without the reviewer; a failed C1 check is a defect for a registered fix, not a host workaround; any deploy recovery, including aisandbox-deploy --from-maintenance or a redeploy of an earlier commit, needs a new decision by Keith. ## DEPLOY-01 AUTH-5c amendment: one more reboot to prove the D1 fix live — 2026-10-28

Under AUTH-5c (repository commits 1968c7264a30b88510010c38dba6ddb4581eda64 and ec4d70e6159c9a61a2ba2d5f9c04cc8fd5f5e181), the deploy of 035040d41fd552a5d278e43647e65120ee61995c passed on 2026-10-28 (01:42:05 to 01:46:20 UTC), checks 1 to 3 passed, and check 4, reboot #3 at 02:02:55 UTC, passed with every unit back, checks 2 and 3 repeated and the C1 repeat history bundle unchanged. Gap found by the reviewer before check 9: the defect D1 path needs a session of the project that is not yet expired and whose container is gone. The last C1 repeat session expired on 2026-10-27, so opening the project now creates a fresh session and never runs the SESSION-STALE-01 code; check 9 as authorized proves D2 and the revert live, but D1 only by tests. On 2026-10-28 Keith approved an amendment to AUTH-5c: after Keith opens C1 repeat in a fresh session and completes the check 9 steps there (ACCEPT.md C1-a, the history view, a revert to C1-b), and within the hour while that session is still valid, one more reboot of ainow-staging-01 (#4) with fresh run markers, the after-reboot checks of check 4, and a reopen of C1 repeat by Keith that must open with no error in a new session after the reused session is ended with termination reason container_lost. Read-only evidence blocks record the session, container, git history, checkpoint ledger and history bundle before the reboot and after the reopen. Everything else in AUTH-5c stands: no other host change, no recovery, no environment-file edit, no email or provider use, nothing on aisandbox-staging, and C2 to D not authorized. The reboot blocks record the history bundle hash only after the check that no reboot was already requested (the AUTH-5c reboot block did it before that check, which would have overwritten the recorded hash on a second run; it was not run twice).

GOVERNANCE is held for this record only and released UNOWNED. The amendment changes no occupancy, mutex, authorization flag, dependency or write scope, so it has no Part 2 and needs no validator run; DEPLOY-01 stays ADMITTED on Lane 1 with STAGING and ENV.
